We derive time-uniform and fixed-time upper tail concentration for
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
.
While the time-uniform result require using the peeling method, the proof of the fixed-time concentration is simpler.
To use the peeling method, we need to control the deviation of the process on slices of time (Lemma
19
).

Lemma 19
.

Let
c>0
and
S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
. Let
N>0
. For all
x>1
, there exists
\lambda=\lambda(x)
such that for all
t\geq N
,

\displaystyle\left\{S_{t}+t\geq tx\right\}\subseteq\left\{\lambda S_{t}-ct\psi_{E,c}(\lambda)\geq\frac{N}{c}\left(h\left(x\right)-1\right)\right\}

where
\lambda(x)=\argmax_{\lambda\in[0,1/c)}\left(x\lambda+\frac{\log(1-c\lambda)}{c}\right)
and
h(x)=x-\log(x)
for
x>1
.

Proof.

Defining
\psi_{U}(\lambda)=\lambda+c\psi_{E,c}(\lambda)=-\frac{\log(1-c\lambda)}{c}
and
\lambda(x)=\argmax_{\lambda\in[0,1/c)}x\lambda-\psi_{U}(\lambda)
, we have
x\lambda(x)-\psi_{U}(\lambda(x))=\psi^{*}_{U}\left(x\right)
where
\psi^{*}_{U}
is the convex conjugate of
\psi_{U}
. Note that
\psi^{*}_{U}\left(x\right)\geq 0
(see below), hence
t\psi^{*}_{U}\left(x\right)\geq N\psi^{*}_{U}\left(x\right)
for
t\geq N
. Direct computations yield

\displaystyle S_{t}+t\geq tx

\displaystyle\iff\lambda S_{t}-ct\psi_{E,c}(\lambda)\geq tx\lambda-t(\lambda+c\psi_{E,c}(\lambda))

\displaystyle\implies\lambda S_{t}-ct\psi_{E,c}(\lambda)\geq t\left(x\lambda-\psi_{U}(\lambda)\right)=t\psi^{*}_{U}\left(x\right)

\displaystyle\implies\lambda S_{t}-ct\psi_{E,c}(\lambda)\geq N_{i}\psi^{*}_{U}\left(x\right)=\frac{N}{c}\left(h\left(x\right)-1\right)

Note that for
f(\lambda)=\lambda x+\frac{\log(1-c\lambda)}{c}
, we have
f^{\prime}(\lambda)=x-\frac{1}{1-c\lambda}=0\iff\lambda=\frac{1}{c}\left(1-\frac{1}{x}\right)
and
\frac{1}{c}\left(1-\frac{1}{x}\right)\in[0,\frac{1}{c})\iff x>1
. Since
f^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)=-\frac{c}{(1-c\lambda)^{2}}\leq 0
, the function is concave hence this is a maximum. This yields that for all
x>1
,
\psi^{*}_{U}(x)=f(\frac{1}{c}\left(1-\frac{1}{x}\right))=\frac{1}{c}\left(x-1-\log(x)\right)=\frac{1}{c}(h(x)-1)\geq 0
where
h(x)=x-\log(x)
.
∎

Let
\eta>0
. Applying Lemma
19
on slices of time with geometric growth rate
(N_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}
with
N_{i}=(1+\eta)^{i-1}
, we obtain Lemma
20
.

Lemma 20
.

Let
\overline{W}_{-1}(x)=-W_{-1}(-e^{-x})
for
x\geq 1
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\eta>0
,
s>1
,
c>0
, and
\zeta
be the Riemann
\zeta
function. Let
S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
. Then, with probability greater than
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
,

\displaystyle S_{t}+t\leq t\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{c(1+\eta)}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta)}\right)\right)\right)\>.

Proof.

Let
g(t,\delta)
such that
g(t,\delta)\geq x_{i}(\delta)
for
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
and
x_{i}(\delta)>1
. Using Lemma
19
with
x_{i}(\delta)>1
and
g(t,\delta)\geq x_{i}(\delta)
on
[N_{i},N_{i+1})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}:S_{t}+t\geq tg(t,\delta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1}):S_{t}+t\geq tx_{i}(\delta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1}):\lambda S_{t}-ct\psi_{E,c}(\lambda)\geq\frac{N_{i}}{c}\left(h\left(x_{i}(\delta)\right)-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}e^{-\frac{N_{i}}{c}\left(h\left(x_{i}(\delta)\right)-1\right)}\>,

where the last inequality uses that
S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
. Taking

g(t,\delta)=\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{c(1+\eta)}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta)}\right)\right)\right)

and
x_{i}(\delta)=\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{c}{N_{i}}\log\left(\frac{i^{s}\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)\right)
satisfies the required properties. First, we have
x_{i}(\delta)>1
(Lemma
75
). Second, since
\overline{W}_{-1}
is increasing on
(1,+\infty)
(Lemma
75
),
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
and
i=1+\frac{\log(N_{i})}{\log(1+\eta)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle g(t,\delta)

\displaystyle\geq\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{c\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+cs\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta)}\right)}{N_{i}}\right)\geq\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{c}{N_{i}}\log\left(\frac{i^{s}\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)\right)

Using Lemma
75
for each
i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}:S_{t}+t\geq tg(t,\delta)\right)\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}e^{-\frac{N_{i}}{c}\left(h\left(x_{i}(\delta)\right)-1\right)}\leq\frac{\delta}{\zeta(s)}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}\frac{1}{i^{s}}=\delta

∎

Fixed-time concentration

When the time is fixed and not random, there is no need to consider slices of time and we can directly control the deviation of the process (Lemma
21
).

Lemma 21
.

Let
h(x)=x-\log(x)
for
x>1
. Let
c>0
and
S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
. Then,

\displaystyle\forall t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star},\>\forall x>1,\quad\mathbb{P}\left(S_{t}+t\geq tx\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t}{c}\left(h\left(x\right)-1\right)\right)\>.

Proof.

With similar computations as in the proof of Lemma
19
, the fact that
S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
and the Chernoff inequality yield the first result.
∎

E.1.2
Lower Tail Concentration

We derive time-uniform and fixed-time lower tail concentration for
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,-c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
.
Likewise, we use the peeling method and control the deviation of the process on slices of time (Lemma
22
).

Lemma 22
.

Let
c>0
and
-S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,-c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
. Let
N>0
. For all
x\in(0,1)
, there exists
\lambda=\lambda(x)
such that for all
t\geq N
,

\displaystyle\left\{-S_{t}-t\geq-tx\right\}\subseteq\left\{\lambda(-S_{t})-ct\psi_{E,-c}(\lambda)\geq\frac{N}{c}\left(h\left(x\right)-1\right)\right\}

where
\lambda(x)=\argmax_{\lambda\in[0,+\infty)}\left(-x\lambda+\frac{\log(1+c\lambda)}{c}\right)
and
h(x)=x-\log(x)
for
x\in(0,1)
.

Proof.

Defining
\psi_{L}(\lambda)=-\lambda+c\psi_{E,-c}(\lambda)=-\frac{\log(1+c\lambda)}{c}
and
\lambda(x)=\argmax_{\lambda\in[0,+\infty)}-x\lambda-\psi_{L}(\lambda)
, we have
-x\lambda(x)-\psi_{L}(\lambda(x))=\psi^{*}_{L}\left(-x\right)\geq 0
(see below), hence
t\psi^{*}_{L}\left(-x\right)\geq N\psi^{*}_{L}\left(-x\right)
for
t\geq N
. Direct computations yield

\displaystyle-S_{t}-t\geq-tx

\displaystyle\iff\lambda(-S_{t})-ct\psi_{E,-c}(\lambda)\geq-tx\lambda-t(-\lambda+c\psi_{E,-c}(\lambda))

\displaystyle\implies\lambda(-S_{t})-ct\psi_{E,-c}(\lambda)\geq t\left(-x\lambda-\psi_{L}(\lambda)\right)=t\psi^{*}_{L}\left(-x\right)

\displaystyle\implies\lambda(-S_{t})-ct\psi_{E,-c}(\lambda)\geq N\psi^{*}_{L}\left(-x\right)=\frac{N}{c}\left(h\left(x\right)-1\right)

Note that for
f(\lambda)=-\lambda x+\frac{\log(1+c\lambda)}{c}
, we have
f^{\prime}(\lambda)=-x+\frac{1}{1+c\lambda}=0\iff\lambda=\frac{1}{c}\left(\frac{1}{x}-1\right)
and
\frac{1}{c}\left(\frac{1}{x}-1\right)\in[0,+\infty)\iff x\in(0,1)
. Since
f^{\prime\prime}(\lambda)=-\frac{c}{(1+c\lambda)^{2}}\leq 0
, the function is concave hence this is a maximum. This yields that for all
x\in(0,1)
,
\psi^{*}_{L}(-x)=f(\frac{1}{c}\left(\frac{1}{x}-1\right))=\frac{1}{c}\left(x-1-\log(x)\right)=\frac{1}{c}\left(h(x)-1\right)\geq 0
where
h(x)=x-\log(x)
for
x\in(0,1)
.
∎

Let
\eta>0
. Applying Lemma
22
on slices of time with geometric growth rate
(N_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}
with
N_{i}=(1+\eta)^{i-1}
, we obtain Lemma
23
.

Lemma 23
.

Let
\overline{W}_{0}(x)=-W_{0}(-e^{-x})
for
x\geq 1
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\eta>0
,
s>1
,
c>0
, and
\zeta
be the Riemann
\zeta
function. Let
S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,-c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
. Then, with probability greater than
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
,

\displaystyle S_{t}+t\geq t\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{c(1+\eta)}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta)}\right)\right)\right)\>.

Proof.

Let
g(t,\delta)
positive such that
g(t,\delta)\leq x_{i}(\delta)
for
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
and
x_{i}(\delta)\in(0,1)
. Using Lemma
22
with
x_{i}(\delta)<1
and
g(t,\delta)\leq x_{i}(\delta)
for
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}:S_{t}+t\leq tg(t,\delta)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in\mathbb{N}:-S_{t}-t\geq-tg(t,\delta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in T_{i}:-S_{t}-t\geq-tx_{i}(\delta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in T_{i}:\lambda(-S_{t})-ct\psi_{E,-c}(\lambda)\geq\frac{N_{i}}{c}\left(h\left(x_{i}(\delta)\right)-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}e^{-\frac{N_{i}}{c}\left(h\left(x_{i}(\delta)\right)-1\right)}

where the last inequality uses that
-S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,-c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
. Taking

g(t,\delta)=\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{c(1+\eta)}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta)}\right)\right)\right)

and
x_{i}(\delta)=\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{c}{N_{i}}\log\left(\frac{i^{s}\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)\right)
satisfies the required properties. First, we have
x_{i}(\delta)\in(0,1)
and
g(t,\delta)>0
(Lemma
75
). Second, since
\overline{W}_{0}
is decreasing on
(1,+\infty)
(Lemma
75
),
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
and
i=1+\frac{\log(N_{i})}{\log(1+\eta)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle g(t,\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{c\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+cs\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta)}\right)}{N_{i}}\right)\leq\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{c}{N_{i}}\log\left(\frac{i^{s}\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)\right)

Using Lemma
75
for each
i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}:S_{t}+t\leq tg(t,\delta)\right)\leq\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}e^{-\frac{N_{i}}{c}\left(h\left(x_{i}(\delta)\right)-1\right)}\leq\frac{\delta}{\zeta(s)}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}\frac{1}{i^{s}}=\delta\>.

∎

Fixed-time concentration

When the time is fixed and not random, there is no need to consider slices of time and we can directly control the deviation of the process (Lemma
24
).

Lemma 24
.

Let
h(x)=x-\log(x)
for
x\in(0,1)
. Let
c>0
and
-S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,-c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
. Then,

\displaystyle\forall t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star},\>\forall x\in(0,1),\quad\mathbb{P}\left(S_{t}+t\leq tx\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t}{c}\left(h\left(x\right)-1\right)\right)\>.

Proof.

With similar computations as in the proof of Lemma
22
, the fact that
-S_{t}
a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,-c}
process with variance process
V_{t}=ct
and the Chernoff inequality yield the first result.
∎

E.2
Univariate Gaussian

We prove time-uniform and fixed-time upper and lower concentration results for the empirical variance (Appendix
E.2.1
) and empirical mean (Appendix
E.2.2
) of Gaussian observations.

E.2.1
Empirical Variance

We first prove Lemma
25
which shows that the empirical variance is closely linked with a sub-exponential process.
This is obtained with manipulations derived in the Appendix H of
Howard et al. 2021
, in which they consider martingales with
\chi^{2}
increments.

Lemma 25
.

Let
\sigma^{2}_{t}
be the empirical variance of
t
i.i.d. samples from a Gaussian distribution with variance
\sigma^{2}
. Then,
\frac{\sigma^{2}_{t}}{\sigma^{2}}=\frac{S_{t-1}-1}{t}+1
with
S_{t-1}+t-1=\sum_{i=1}^{t-1}Y_{i}^{2}
where
(Y_{i})
are i.i.d. with distributions
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. In particular,
S_{t}
is a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,2}
process and
-S_{t}
is a
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,-2}
process, both with variance process
V_{t}=2t
.

Proof.

Let
(X_{i})_{i\in[n]}
the
n
samples from a Gaussian distribution with parameters
(\mu,\sigma^{2})
. Let
\hat{\mu}_{n}
and
\hat{\sigma}_{n}^{2}
be the empirical mean and variance.
Let
Z_{i}=\frac{X_{i}-\mu}{\sigma}
for all
i\in[n]
,
\hat{Z}_{n}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}Z_{i}
and
S_{n-1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(Z_{i}-\hat{Z}_{n})^{2}-(n-1)
.
Then,
S_{0}=0
and for all
n\geq 2

\displaystyle S_{n-1}=\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(X_{i}-\hat{\mu}_{n})^{2}-(n-1)=n\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}-(n-1)\>.

Rewriting the increment of
S_{n}
, we obtain for all
n\geq 2

\displaystyle S_{n-1}-S_{n-2}

\displaystyle=(Z_{n}-\hat{Z}_{n})^{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}((Z_{i}-\hat{Z}_{n})^{2}-(Z_{i}-\hat{Z}_{n-1})^{2})-1

\displaystyle=Z_{n}^{2}+\hat{Z}_{n}^{2}-2Z_{n}\hat{Z}_{n}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(-2Z_{i}\hat{Z}_{n}+2Z_{i}\hat{Z}_{n-1})+(n-1)(\hat{Z}_{n}^{2}-\hat{Z}_{n-1}^{2})-1

\displaystyle=Z_{n}^{2}-n\hat{Z}_{n}^{2}+(n-1)\hat{Z}_{n-1}^{2}-1=\frac{n-1}{n}(Z_{n}-\hat{Z}_{n-1})^{2}-1\>.

Since
S_{n-1}=\sum_{s=1}^{n-1}(S_{s}-S_{s-1})
and
S_{0}=0
a.s., we obtain
S_{n-1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(Y_{i}^{2}-1)
where
Y_{n-1}=\sqrt{\frac{n-1}{n}}(Z_{n}-\hat{Z}_{n-1})
. The
(Y_{i})
are iid with distribution
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
and the CGF of
(Y_{i}^{2}-1)
is

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}e^{\lambda(Y_{i}^{2}-1)}=-\frac{\log(1-2\lambda)}{2}-\lambda=2\psi_{E,2}(\lambda)\quad\text{for }\lambda\in(-\infty,1/2)\>.

By Definition
17
, we have that
S_{n}
is
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,2}
and that
-S_{n}
is
1
-sub-
\psi_{E,-2}
, both with variance process
V_{n}=2(n-1)
.
∎

Thanks to Lemmas
20
-
23
-
25
, Corollary
26
gives time-uniform upper and lower tails concentrations on the empirical variance of Gaussian observation.

Corollary 26
.

For
i\in\{0,-1\}
, let
\overline{W}_{i}(x)=-W_{i}(-e^{-x})
for
x\geq 1
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\eta_{0},\eta_{1}>0
,
s>1
and
\zeta
be the Riemann
\zeta
function. Let
\sigma^{2}_{t+1}
be the empirical variance of
t+1
i.i.d. samples from a Gaussian distribution with variance
\sigma^{2}
. Then, with probability greater than
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{t+1}\leq\sigma^{2}\left(\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{2(1+\eta_{1})}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta_{1})}\right)\right)\right)-\frac{1}{t}\right)\>.

Moreover, with probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\geq t_{0}(\delta)
,

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}_{t+1}\geq\sigma^{2}\left(\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{2(1+\eta_{0})}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta_{0})}\right)\right)\right)-\frac{1}{t}\right)\>,

where the initial time condition, which ensures the lower bound is positive, is

t_{0}(\delta)=\inf\left\{t\mid t>e^{1+W_{0}\left(\frac{2(1+\eta_{0})}{e}\left(\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta_{0})}\right)\right)-e^{-1}\right)}\right\}\>.

Proof.

Combining Lemmas
20
-
23
-
25
yields the desired result. Using Lemma
75
, we know that the upper bound is always positive. The intial time condition, after which the lower bound is positive, is obtained by Lemma
75

\displaystyle\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{2(1+\eta_{0})}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta_{0})}\right)\right)\right)>\frac{1}{t}\quad\iff\quad t\geq t_{0}(\delta)\>.

∎

Fixed-time concentration

To our knowledge, the first fixed-time upper tail concentration result for the empirical variance dates back to Lemma 3 of
Honda and Takemura 2014
and to Lemma 7 of
Chan 2020
for fixed-time lower tail concentration results.
Corollary
27
is obtained as a direct consequence of Lemmas
21
-
24
-
25
, hence the proof is omitted.

Corollary 27
.

Let
h(x)=x-\log(x)
for
x>0
. Let
\sigma^{2}_{t+1}
be the empirical variance of
t+1
i.i.d. samples from a Gaussian distribution with variance
\sigma^{2}
. Then,

\displaystyle\forall t\geq 1,\>\forall x>1,\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma^{2}_{t+1}\geq\sigma^{2}x\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t}{2}\left(h\left(x+\frac{1}{t}\right)-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle\forall t\geq 1,\>\forall x\in\left(0,1-\frac{1}{t}\right),\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\sigma^{2}_{t+1}\leq\sigma^{2}x\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{t}{2}\left(h\left(x+\frac{1}{t}\right)-1\right)\right)\>.

On sub-Gaussian distributions with unknown variances

While it is well-known that (time-uniform) concentration for Gaussian distributions with known
\sigma^{2}
apply to
\sigma^{2}
-sub-Gaussian distributions, thus the same bandit algorithms can be used in both settings, we believe that there is no counterpart of this phenomenon when
\sigma^{2}
is unknown.
For regret minimization, some papers have provided examples of sub-Gaussian arms with unknown
\sigma^{2}
under which the regret can be linear.

Extending our algorithms to sub-Gaussian distributions would require time-uniform concentration results for the empirical variance of sub-Gaussian distributions with unknown variance.
Our concentration on the empirical variance relies on Lemma
25
, which leverages the fact that the empirical mean and empirical variances of Gaussian distributions are independent, which does not extend to the sub-Gaussian case.

Moreover, if the focus is on asymptotically optimal algorithms, we note that it is difficult to express the characteristic time
T^{\star}
for the non-parametric class of sub-Gaussian distributions with unknown variances.
Optimal BAI has however been studied under other interesting non-parametric assumptions (see, e.g.,
Agrawal et al. 2020
).

E.2.2
Empirical Mean

While time-uniform concentration results for the empirical mean of Gaussian observations already exist in the literature (e.g.
Kaufmann and Koolen 2021
), Lemma
28
is proved for completeness and to present unified concentration results as it also involves
\overline{W}_{-1}
. The empirical mean of a Gaussian after a given number of observations is a sub-Gaussian random variable.
It is in fact exactly Gaussian, but the sub-Gaussian hypothesis will be easier to handle for a random number of samples.

Lemma 28
.

Let
\overline{W}_{-1}(x)=-W_{-1}(-e^{-x})
for
x\geq 1
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
s>1
and
\zeta
be the Riemann
\zeta
function. Let
\mu_{t}
be the empirical mean of
t
i.i.d. samples from a Gaussian distribution with parameter
(\mu,\sigma^{2})
. Then, with probability greater than
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
,

\displaystyle|\mu_{t}-\mu|\leq\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{t}\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+2\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+2g(s)+2s\log\left(2s+\log t\right)\right)}\>,

where
g(s)=\log(\zeta(s))+s(1-\log(2s))
.

Proof.

Let
(X_{s})_{s\in[t]}
the observations from a standard normal distributions and denote
S_{t}=\sum_{s\in[t]}X_{s}
. We will derive a concentration result for
S_{t}=t\frac{\hat{\mu}_{t}-\mu}{\sigma}
, which implies a concentration result for
\hat{\mu}_{t}
.

Let
\eta>0
and
s>1
.
For all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
, let
\gamma_{i}>0
and
N_{i}=(1+\eta)^{i-1}
.
For all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
, we define the family of priors
f_{N_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x)=\sqrt{\frac{\gamma_{i}N_{i}}{2\pi}}\exp\left(-\frac{x^{2}\gamma_{i}N_{i}}{2}\right)
with weights
w_{i}=\frac{1}{i^{s}\zeta(s)}
and process

\displaystyle\overline{M}(t)=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}w_{i}\int f_{N_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x)\exp\left(xS_{t}-\frac{1}{2}x^{2}t\right)\,dx\>,

which satisfies
\overline{M}(0)=1
since
\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}w_{i}
and
\int f_{N_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x)\,dx=1
. A test martingale is defined as a non-negative martingale of unit initial value.
It is direct to see that
M(t)=\exp\left(xS_{t}-\frac{1}{2}x^{2}t\right)
is a test martingale, as
M(0)=1
and
\expectedvalue[M(t)\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]=M(t-1)\expectedvalue_{Y\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}[e^{xY-\frac{1}{2}x^{2}}]=M(t-1)
since
\expectedvalue_{Y\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)}[e^{xY}]=e^{\frac{1}{2}x^{2}}
.
By Tonelli’s theorem and using that
M(t)
is a martingale

\displaystyle\expectedvalue[\overline{M}(t)\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}w_{i}\int f_{N_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x)M(t-1)\,dx=\overline{M}(t-1)\>.

Therefore,
\overline{M}(t)
is also a test martingale. Let
i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
and consider
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
. For all
x
,

f_{N_{i},\gamma}(x)\geq\sqrt{\frac{N_{i}}{t}}f_{t,\gamma_{i}}(x)\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\eta}}f_{t,\gamma_{i}}(x)

Direct computations shows that

\displaystyle\int f_{t,\gamma_{i}}(x)\exp\left(xS_{t}-\frac{1}{2}x^{2}t\right)\,dx=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\gamma_{i}^{-1}}}\exp\left(\frac{S_{t}^{2}}{2(1+\gamma_{i})t}\right)\>.

Combining those results with the fact that
\overline{M}(t)\geq w_{i}\int f_{N_{i},\gamma_{i}}(x)\exp\left(xS_{t}-\frac{1}{2}x^{2}t\right)\,dx
, we obtain

\displaystyle\overline{M}(t)\geq\frac{1}{i^{s}\zeta(s)}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1+\gamma_{i}^{-1})(1+\eta)}}\exp\left(\frac{S_{t}^{2}}{2(1+\gamma_{i})t}\right)\>,

Using Ville’s maximal inequality, we have that with probability greater than
1-\delta
,
\log\overline{M}(t)\leq\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)
. Therefore, with probability greater than
1-\delta
, for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
and
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
,

\displaystyle\frac{|S_{t}|}{\sqrt{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{(1+\gamma_{i})\left(2\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+2\log\left(i^{s}\zeta(s)\right)+\log(1+\gamma_{i}^{-1})+\log(1+\eta)\right)}\>.

Since this upper bound is independent of
t
, we can optimize it and choose
\gamma_{i}
as in Lemma
76
for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
.
Therefore, with probability greater than
1-\delta
, for all
i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
and
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
,

\displaystyle\frac{S_{t}^{2}}{t}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+2\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+2s\log\left(i\right)+\log(1+\eta)\right)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+2\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+2s\log\left(\log(1+\eta)+\log t\right)-2s\log\log(1+\eta)+\log(1+\eta)\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+2\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+2s\log\left(2s+\log t\right)+2g(s)\right)

where
g(s)=\log(\zeta(s))+s(1-\log(2s))
. The second inequality is obtained since
i\leq 1+\frac{\log t}{\log(1+\eta)}
for
t\in[N_{i},N_{i+1})
. The last equality is obtained for the choice
\eta^{\star}=e^{2s}-1
, which minimizes
\eta\mapsto\log(1+\eta)-2s\log(\log(1+\eta))
. Since
\mathbb{N}^{\star}\subseteq\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}}[N_{i},N_{i+1})
and
\frac{\hat{\mu}_{t}-\mu}{\sigma}=\frac{S_{t}}{t}
this yields the result.
∎

Fixed-time concentration

Lemma
29
is a known result for the deviation of the empirical mean of Gaussian observations, hence we omit the proof (see Ex. 2.2.23 in
Dembo and Zeitouni 1998
).

Lemma 29
.

Let
\mu_{t}
be the empirical mean of
t
i.i.d. samples from a Gaussian distribution with parameter
(\mu,\sigma^{2})
. Then, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}^{\star}
and all
x>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\mu_{t}\geq\mu+x\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{tx^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\forall x>0,\quad\mathbb{P}\left(\mu_{t}\leq\mu-x\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{tx^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)\>.

Appendix F
Kullback-Leibler Concentration

We prove our time-uniform upper tail concentration for
d
-dimensional exponential families (Theorem
42
).
The two key novelties compared to previous work
Degenne 2019
are that we consider: (1) a sum over
\mathcal{S}\subseteq[K]
arms and (2)
\Theta_{D}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
To go from one arm to
\mathcal{S}
arms, it is enough to consider the product of the the mixture of martingales used for each arm.
Dealing with a support different from
\mathbb{R}^{d}
is the real challenge of the proof.
This is the reason why we start by showing the result for one arm in Appendix
F.1
, and then generalize it to
\mathcal{S}
arms in Appendix
F.2
.
For Gaussian with unknown variance, which are a
2
-dimensional exponential family with support
\Theta_{D}=\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}_{-}^{\star}
, we obtain Theorem
43
(Appendix
F.3
).

In the following, each arm
a\in[K]
has a parameter
\theta_{a}
and distribution
\nu_{\theta_{a}}
belonging to an exponential family with parameter space
\Theta_{a}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
(we call
d
the dimension of the family), sufficient statistic
F_{a}:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and log-partition function
\phi_{a}:\Theta_{a}\to\mathbb{R}
. That is, there exists a distribution
\nu_{0}
such that
\nu_{\theta_{a}}
is defined by

\frac{d\nu_{\theta_{a}}}{d\nu_{0}}(X)=\exp(\theta_{a}^{\top}F_{a}(X)-\phi_{a}(\theta_{a}))\>.

We define the average statistic
F_{t,a}:=\frac{1}{N_{t,a}}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}\{a_{s}=a\}F_{a}(X_{s})
.
We denote the maximum likelihood estimator (MLE) of
\theta_{a}
by
\theta_{t,a}
. It is defined as
\theta_{t,a}:=\argmax_{\lambda\in\Theta_{a}}\lambda^{\top}F_{t,a}-\phi_{a}(\lambda)
(and may not exist).
When
F_{t,a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{a})
, we have
\theta_{t,a}=(\nabla\phi_{a})^{-1}(F_{t,a})
.

Let
d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\lambda_{a})
denote the Bregman divergence of
\phi_{a}
between parameters
\theta_{a}
and
\lambda_{a}
. It is equal to the Kullback-Leibler divergence between the distributions with parameters
\lambda_{a}
and
\theta_{a}
(note the reversed order of the parameters). For a subset of arms
\mathcal{S}\subseteq[K]
, we seek high probability bounds on
\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{t,a})
.

F.1
One Arm

In this section we consider one arm
a\in[K]
. The first step of the proof consists in linking the KL divergence to a mixture of martingales in order to obtain a time-uniform upper tail concentration (Lemma
30
).

Lemma 30
.

Let
\rho_{0,a}
be a distribution supported on
\Theta_{D,a}
and
\tau
be an almost surely bounded stopping time. With probability
1-\delta
, either
F_{N_{\tau,a},a}\notin\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
or

\displaystyle N_{\tau,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{\tau,a},a})\leq-\log\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\exp\left(-N_{\tau,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(y,\theta_{N_{\tau,a},a})\right)+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\>.

Proof.

We first remark that for all
y\in\Theta_{D,a}
, the log-likelihood ratio
\prod_{s\leq t,a_{s}=a}\frac{d\nu_{y}}{d\nu_{\theta_{a}}}(X_{s})
is a martingale with expectation 1 under
\nu_{\theta_{a}}
. This is also true for
\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\left[\prod_{s\leq t,a_{s}=a}\frac{d\nu_{y}}{d\nu_{\theta_{a}}}(X_{s})\right]
. By the optional stopping theorem, its stopped version at
\tau
also has expectation 1. Then by Markov’s inequality, with probability
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\left[\prod_{s\leq\tau,a_{s}=a}\frac{d\nu_{y}}{d\nu_{\theta_{a}}}(X_{s})\right]\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\>.

(15)

We now rewrite the product as
\exp\left(\sum_{s=1}^{\tau}\mathbb{I}\{a_{s}=a\}\log\frac{d\nu_{y}}{d\nu_{\theta_{a}}}(X_{s})\right)
and detail its value:

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbb{I}\{a_{s}=a\}\log\frac{d\nu_{y}}{d\nu_{\theta_{a}}}(X_{s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbb{I}\{a_{s}=a\}(\phi_{a}(\theta_{a})-\phi_{a}(y)-(\theta_{a}-y)^{\top}F_{a}(X_{s}))

\displaystyle=N_{t,a}(\phi_{a}(\theta_{a})-\phi_{a}(y)-(\theta_{a}-y)^{\top}F_{N_{t,a},a})\>.

Now if
F_{N_{\tau,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
, we have
F_{N_{t,a},a}=\nabla\phi_{a}(\theta_{N_{t,a},a})
and

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbb{I}\{a_{s}=a\}\log\frac{d\nu_{y}}{d\nu_{\theta_{a}}}(X_{s})

\displaystyle=N_{t,a}(\phi_{a}(\theta_{a})-\phi_{a}(y)-(\theta_{a}-y)^{\top}\nabla\phi_{a}(\theta_{N_{t,a},a}))

\displaystyle=N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})-N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(y,\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\>.

We can write (
15
) again with that expression to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\exp\left(N_{\tau,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{\tau,a},a})-N_{\tau,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(y,\theta_{N_{\tau,a},a})\right)\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\>.

The first term in the subtraction does not depend on
y
and ca be brought outside of the expectation. Taking the logarithm then proves the lemma.
∎

Corollary
31
generalizes Lemma
30
to all times.

Corollary 31
.

Let
\rho_{0,a}
be a distribution supported on
\Theta_{D,a}
. With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, either
F_{N_{t,a},a}\notin\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
or

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\leq-\log\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\exp\left(-N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(y,\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\right)+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\>.

Proof.

The extension of the result of Lemma
30
from a stopping time to all times is standard.
∎

We will now use Corollary
31
with a truncated Gaussian prior (Lemma
32
). The reason for the truncation is that the domain
\Theta_{D,a}
may not be
\mathbb{R}^{d}
but only a subset. In that case, we have to restrict the prior. We also truncate to ensure a control the hessian
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}
on the support with positive semi-definite upper and lower bound.

For
M
a positive-definite matrix and a set
S
, let
V_{M}(S)
be the volume of set
S
according to measure
\mathcal{N}(0,M^{-1})
. Note that
V_{M}(S)=V_{I_{d}}(M^{1/2}S)
.

Lemma 32
.

Let
A
be a convex set containing 0,
H
and
G
be upper bounds and lower bounds of
\nabla^{2}\phi
in the positive semi-definite (PSD) sense on
\theta_{a}+A
. Let
M_{0}
be a positive definite matrix.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\xi_{n,a}=((nH)^{-1}+M_{0}^{-1})^{-1}(nH\theta_{n,a}+M_{0}\theta_{a})
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A
then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\theta_{a}-\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\|_{((N_{t,a}H)^{-1}+M_{0}^{-1})^{-1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\log\det(1+N_{t,a}HM_{0}^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad+\log\frac{1}{\delta}+\log\frac{V_{M_{0}}(A)}{V_{N_{t,a}H+M_{0}}(\theta_{a}-\xi_{N_{t,a},a}+A)}\>.

Proof.

We use Corollary
31
with prior
\rho_{0,a}
equal to
\mathcal{N}(\theta_{a},M_{0}^{-1})
truncated to
A
and rescaled to have mass 1.

Suppose that
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A
. For all
y\in\theta_{a}+A
, since
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A
as well and
A
is convex, we can conclude from the definition of
H
that
d_{\phi_{a}}(y,\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\leq\frac{1}{2}\|y-\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\|_{H}^{2}
. We use this to compute an upper bound to the expectation in Corollary
31
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\left[\exp\left(-nd_{\phi_{a}}\left(y,\theta_{n,a}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\left[\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|y-\theta_{n}\|_{nH}^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\det(M_{0})}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}V_{M_{0}}(A)}

\displaystyle\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\theta+A}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|y-\theta\|_{M_{0}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|y-\theta_{n}\|_{nH}^{2}\right)\,dy

Let
\xi_{n}=(nH+M_{0})^{-1}(nH\theta_{n}+M_{0}\theta_{a})
and
C_{n}=\frac{1}{2}\|\theta_{a}-\theta_{n}\|_{((nH)^{-1}+M_{0}^{-1})^{-1}}^{2}
. Remark that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\|y-\theta\|_{M_{0}}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|y-\theta_{n}\|_{nH}^{2}=-\frac{1}{2}\|y-\xi_{n}\|_{nH+M_{0}}^{2}-C_{n}\>.

We use this equality:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\left[\exp\left(-nd_{\phi}\left(y,\theta_{n}\right)\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sqrt{\det(M_{0})}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}V_{M_{0}}(A)}e^{-C_{n}}\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\theta_{a}+A}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|y-\xi_{n}\|_{nH+M_{0}}^{2}\right)\,dy

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{\det(M_{0})}}{\sqrt{\det(nH+M_{0})}}e^{-C_{n}}\frac{V_{nH+M_{0}}(\theta_{a}-\xi_{n}+A)}{V_{M_{0}}(A)}\>.

The result of the lemma is obtained by taking the logarithm of this expression.
∎

Lemma
33
starts by choosing for
H
and
G
multiples of the identity, hence we will compare the divergence
d_{\phi_{a}}
with the euclidean distance
(x,y)\mapsto\|x-y\|^{2}/2
.
This choice is possible by using the maximal and minimal eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)
on
A
, which we denote by

\displaystyle\lambda_{+,a}

\displaystyle=\max\left\{\lambda_{+}\left(\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\tilde{\theta}_{a})\right)\mid\tilde{\theta}_{a}\in\theta_{a}+A\right\}\>,\>\lambda_{-,a}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\lambda_{-}\left(\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\tilde{\theta}_{a})\right)\mid\tilde{\theta}_{a}\in\theta_{a}+A\right\}\>.

For
v>0
and a set
S
, let
V_{v}(S)
be the volume of set
S
according to measure
\mathcal{N}(0,v^{-1}I_{d})
.

Lemma 33
.

Let
A
be a convex set containing 0,
\lambda_{+,a}
and
\lambda_{-,a}
be the maximum and minimum of the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)
on
\theta_{a}+A
and let
v_{0}>0
.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\kappa_{n}=\frac{v_{0}}{n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0}}
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A
then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\theta_{a}-\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\|^{2}\frac{1}{(N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a})^{-1}+v_{0}^{-1}}+\frac{d}{2}\log(1+N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a}v_{0}^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad+\log\frac{1}{\delta}+\log\frac{V_{v_{0}}(A)}{V_{N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0}}(\kappa_{N_{t,a}}A)}\>.

Note that since
\rho_{0,a}
is a prior and has to ensure the martingale property used in the proof of Lemma
30
, it cannot depend on the observations and in particular it cannot depend on the random variable
N_{t,a}
.

Proof.

We use Corollary
31
with prior
\rho_{0,a}
equal to
\mathcal{N}(\theta_{a},v_{0}^{-1}I_{d})
truncated to
A
and rescaled to have mass 1.

Suppose that
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A
. As in Lemma
32
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\left[e^{-nd_{\phi}\left(y,\theta_{n}\right)}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{v_{0}^{d/2}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}V_{v_{0}}(A)}e^{-C_{n}}\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\theta_{a}+A}\exp\left(-\frac{n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0}}{2}\|y-\xi_{n}\|^{2}\right)\,dy\>.

We define
\kappa_{n}=\frac{v_{0}}{n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0}}
and show that
\xi_{n}+\kappa_{n}A\subseteq\theta_{a}+A
. We can rewrite
\xi_{n}=\kappa_{n}\theta_{a}+(1-\kappa_{n})\theta_{n}
.
Let then
y\in\xi_{n}+\kappa_{n}A
. We need to prove that
y-\theta_{a}\in A
, but
y-\theta_{a}=y-\xi_{n}+(1-\kappa_{n})(\theta_{n}-\theta_{a})
. By hypothesis
y-\xi_{n}\in\kappa_{n}A
and
(1-\kappa_{n})(\theta_{n}-\theta_{a})\in(1-\kappa_{n})A
. By convexity of
A
, we conclude that
\kappa_{n}A+(1-\kappa_{n})A\subseteq A
and
y\in\theta_{a}+A
.

We use that set inclusion to lower bound the integral over
\theta_{a}+A
by the same integral over the subset
\xi_{n}+\kappa_{n}A
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\left[e^{-nd_{\phi}\left(y,\theta_{n}\right)}\right]\geq\frac{v_{0}^{d/2}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}V_{v_{0}}(A)}e^{-C_{n}}\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\xi_{n}+\kappa_{n}A}\exp\left(-\frac{n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0}}{2}\|y-\xi_{n}\|^{2}\right)\,dy

\displaystyle=\frac{v_{0}^{d/2}e^{-C_{n}}}{(n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0})^{d/2}V_{v_{0}}(A)}\frac{(n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0})^{d/2}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}}\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\xi_{n}+\kappa_{n}A}\exp\left(-\frac{n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0}}{2}\|y-\xi_{n}\|^{2}\right)\,dy

\displaystyle=e^{-C_{n}}\frac{v_{0}^{d/2}}{(n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0})^{d/2}}\frac{V_{n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0}}(\kappa_{n}A)}{V_{v_{0}}(A)}\>.

The result of the lemma is obtained by taking the logarithm of this expression.
∎

Lemma
34
gives a bound on the ratio of volumes.

Lemma 34
.

Let
\lambda,n,v>0
and
\kappa_{n}=\frac{v}{n\lambda+v}
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{V_{v}(A)}{V_{n\lambda+v}(\kappa_{n}A)}\leq\kappa_{n}^{-d/2}\>.

Proof.

We first prove that for all
\alpha\geq 1
,
\frac{V_{1}(\alpha A)}{V_{1}(A)}\leq\alpha^{d}
.
For a convex set
S\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
containing 0, let
\gamma_{S}(x)=\argmax\{r\geq 0\mid rx\in S\}
. We have that for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{\star}
,
\gamma_{S}(\beta y)\beta y=\gamma_{S}(y)y
. Note also that
\gamma_{S}(x)\geq 1\iff x\in S
.

\displaystyle\frac{V_{1}(\alpha A)}{V_{1}(A)}=1+\frac{V_{1}(\alpha A\setminus A)}{V_{1}(A)}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha A\setminus A}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dx}{\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{A}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dx}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\frac{\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha A\setminus A}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma_{A}(x)x\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dx}{\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{A}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma_{A}(x)x\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dx}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha A}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma_{A}(x)x\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dx}{\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{A}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma_{A}(x)x\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dx}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha^{d}\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{A}(y)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma_{A}(\alpha y)\alpha y\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dy}{\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{A}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma_{A}(x)x\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dx}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha^{d}\int_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{A}(y)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma_{A}(y)y\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dy}{\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbf{1}_{A}(x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\gamma_{A}(x)x\|^{2}_{2}\right)\,dx}=\alpha^{d}\>.

Using that
\frac{\sqrt{v}}{\sqrt{n\lambda+v}\kappa_{n}}=\kappa_{n}^{-1/2}\geq 1
, we obtain the result

\displaystyle\frac{V_{v}(A)}{V_{n\lambda+v}(\kappa_{n}A)}

\displaystyle=\frac{V_{1}(\sqrt{v}A)}{V_{1}(\sqrt{n\lambda+v}\kappa_{n}A)}\leq\left(\frac{\sqrt{v}}{\sqrt{n\lambda+v}\kappa_{n}}\right)^{d}=\kappa_{n}^{-d/2}\>.

∎

Combining Lemma
33
and Lemma
34
yield Corollary
35
.

Corollary 35
.

Let
A
be a convex set containing 0,
\lambda_{+,a}
and
\lambda_{-,a}
be the maximum and minimum of the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)
on
\theta_{a}+A
and let
v_{0}>0
.
For
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\kappa_{n}=\frac{v_{0}}{n\lambda_{+,a}+v_{0}}
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A
then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\theta_{a}-\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\|^{2}\frac{1}{(N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a})^{-1}+v_{0}^{-1}}+\frac{d}{2}\log(1+N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a}v_{0}^{-1})

\displaystyle\quad+\log\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{d}{2}\log\kappa_{N_{t,a}}^{-1}\>.

Proof.

This is simply Lemma
33
combined with the bound on the ratio of volumes of Lemma
34
.
∎

We will now choose
\gamma>1
and define values
n_{i}=\gamma^{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{N}
. For each interval
[n_{i},n_{i+1})
, we will choose a good value of the variance
v_{0}^{-1}
to get a bound for
N_{t,a}
in this interval. The reason we restrict to an interval is that the best choice for that variance scales with
N_{t,a}
, but we are not allowed to do that since the prior cannot depend on
N_{t,a}
. Thanks to this smart choice, we can control the KL divergence on this interval (Lemma
36
).

The ratio of the eigenvalues represents the approximation error in considering that
d_{\phi_{a}}
is locally quadratic. It yields that
\lambda_{-,a}I_{d}\preceq\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)\preceq\lambda_{+,a}I_{d}
on the set, which for
x,y\in\Theta_{a}
translates to

\lambda_{-,a}\leq d_{\phi_{a}}(x,y)/(\|x-y\|^{2}/2)\leq\lambda_{+,a}\ :.

Lemma 36
.

Let
A
be a convex set containing 0,
\lambda_{+,a}
and
\lambda_{-,a}
be the maximum and minimum of the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)
on
\theta_{a}+A
and let
\eta>0
,
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
,
N_{t,a}\in[n_{i},n_{i+1})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A
then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\leq(1+\eta)\left(d\log(1+\frac{1}{\eta})+d\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}})+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\>.

Proof.

We assume in this proof that the three conditions of the lemma are true.

Choose
v_{0,i}^{-1}=\frac{1}{\lambda_{-,a}n_{i}}((1+\frac{1}{\eta})-\frac{\lambda_{-,a}}{\lambda_{+,a}})
. Then for
N_{t,a}\in[n_{i},n_{i+1})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a})^{-1}+v_{0,i}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a})^{-1}+\frac{1}{\lambda_{-,a}N_{t,a}}((1+\frac{1}{\eta})-\frac{\lambda_{-,a}}{\lambda_{+,a}})}=N_{t,a}\lambda_{-,a}(1-\frac{1}{1+\eta})\>,

\displaystyle\log(1+N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a}v_{0,i}^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq\log(1+\gamma n_{i}\lambda_{+,a}v_{0,i}^{-1})

\displaystyle=\log(1-\gamma+\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}}(1+\frac{1}{\eta}))\leq\log(1+\frac{1}{\eta})+\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}})\>.

With that value of
v_{0,i}
, we also have

\displaystyle\kappa_{N_{t,a}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=1+N_{t,a}\lambda_{+,a}v_{0,i}^{-1}\leq 1+\gamma n_{i}\lambda_{+,a}v_{0,i}^{-1}=1-\gamma+\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}}(1+\frac{1}{\eta})

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}}(1+\frac{1}{\eta})\>.

We use those inequalities in Corollary
35
to get

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|\theta_{a}-\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\|^{2}N_{t,a}\lambda_{-,a}(1-\frac{1}{1+\eta})+d\log(1+\frac{1}{\eta})+d\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}})+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\>.

Thanks to the fact that
\lambda_{-,a}
is a lower bound on the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}
on
\theta_{a}+A
, we have
\frac{1}{2}\|\theta_{a}-\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\|^{2}\lambda_{-,a}\leq d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})
. We group the divergences on the left of the inequality to finally obtain

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\leq(1+\eta)\left(d\log(1+\frac{1}{\eta})+d\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}})+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)\>.

∎

A good choice of
\eta
yields Lemma
37
.

Lemma 37
.

Let
A
be a convex set containing 0,
\lambda_{+,a}
and
\lambda_{-,a}
be the maximum and minimum of the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}
on
\theta_{a}+A
and let
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
,
N_{t,a}\in[n_{i},n_{i+1})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A
then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{d}\log\frac{1}{\delta}+\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}})\right)\>.

Proof.

Optimize
\eta
in Lemma
36
by using Lemma
76
.
∎

Considering a union bound over the intervals and applying Lemma
37
, we obtain Lemma
38
. Therefore, we have obtained a time-uniform upper tail concentration of the KL divergence of a
d
-dimensional exponential family with support
\Theta_{D}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Lemma 38
.

Let
(A_{a}(n,\delta))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of non-increasing convex sets containing 0,
\lambda_{+,a}(n,\delta)
and
\lambda_{-,a}(n,\delta)
be the maximum and minimum of the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)
on
\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n,\delta)
. Let
s>1
and let
\zeta
be the Riemann
\zeta
function. For all
t
, let
i(t)=\lfloor\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a}\rfloor
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(N_{t,a},\delta)
, then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\log\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}+\frac{s}{d}\log(1+\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a})+\log\left(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i(t)},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i(t)},\delta)}\right)\right)\>.

Proof.

Suppose that
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
.

For all
i\in\mathbb{N}
, Lemma
37
gives that with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{\zeta(s)(i+1)^{s}}
, if
N_{t,a}\in[n_{i},n_{i+1})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n_{i},\delta)
, then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{d}\log\frac{\zeta(s)(i+1)^{s}}{\delta}+\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i},\delta)})\right)\>.

With probability
1-\delta
, this is true for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular we have the inequality for
i(t)
: if
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n_{i(t)},\delta)
, then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{d}\log\frac{\zeta(s)(i(t)+1)^{s}}{\delta}+\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i(t)},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i(t)},\delta)})\right)\>.

Since the sets
A_{a}(n,\delta)
are non-increasing, a sufficient condition for
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n_{i(t)},\delta)
is
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(N_{t,a},\delta)
.
By definition of
i(t)
, it satisfies
i(t)\leq\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a}
. We get that if
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(N_{t,a},\delta)
, then

\displaystyle N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{d}\log\frac{\zeta(s)}{\delta}+\frac{s}{d}\log(1+\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a})+\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i(t)},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i(t)},\delta)})\right)\>.

∎

To obtain Theorem
42
, it remains to extend this argument to a sum of
\mathcal{S}\subseteq[K]
arms.

F.2
Sum Over Arms

In this section we consider s subset of arms
\mathcal{S}\subseteq[K]
. Lemma
39
extends Corollary
31
to the sum over
\mathcal{S}
arms.

Lemma 39
.

Let
(\rho_{0,a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}
be distributions each supported on
\Theta_{D,a}
. With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, either there exists
a\in\mathcal{S}
such that
F_{N_{t,a},a}\notin\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
or

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\leq-\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log\mathbb{E}_{y\sim\rho_{0,a}}\exp\left(-N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(y,\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\right)+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\>.

Proof.

The proof is the same as the one of Lemma
30
and Corollary
31
, except that the likelihood ratio martingale considered contains observations of all arms in
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

Let
\gamma>1
. We define
n_{i}=\gamma^{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Lemma
40
extends Lemma
36
the sum over
\mathcal{S}
arms.

Lemma 40
.

Let
(A_{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}
be convex sets containing 0,
\lambda_{+,a}
and
\lambda_{-,a}
be the maximum and minimum of the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)
on
A_{a}
and let
\eta>0
,
(i^{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{N}^{|\mathcal{S}|}
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
,
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
,
N_{t,a}\in[n_{i^{a}},n_{i^{a}+1})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}
then

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})\leq(1+\eta)\left(d|\mathcal{S}|\log(1+\frac{1}{\eta})+\log\frac{1}{\delta}+d\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}})\right)\>.

Proof.

For each arm, we choose a truncated Gaussian prior, as in Lemma
32
, and consider upper and lower bound multiples of the identity, as in Lemma
33
. Then, we choose the variance of that prior according to the interval, as in Lemma
36
.
∎

Lemma
41
extends Lemma
37
the sum over
\mathcal{S}
arms.

Lemma 41
.

Let
(A_{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}
be convex sets containing 0,
\lambda_{+,a}
and
\lambda_{-,a}
be the maximum and minimum of the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)
on
\theta_{a}+A_{a}
and let
(i^{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{N}^{|\mathcal{S}|}
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
,
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
,
N_{t,a}\in[n_{i^{a}},n_{i^{a}+1})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}
then

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d|\mathcal{S}|\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{d|\mathcal{S}|}\log\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}}{\lambda_{-,a}})\right)\>.

Proof.

Optimize
\eta
in Lemma
40
by using Lemma
76
.
∎

Theorem
42
extends Lemma
38
to the sum of
\mathcal{S}
arms. Therefore, we have obtained a time-uniform upper tail concentration of the sum of
\mathcal{S}
KL divergence of
d
-dimensional exponential family with support
\Theta_{D,a}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Theorem 42
.

For all
a\in\mathcal{S}
, let
(A_{a}(n,\delta))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of non-increasing convex sets containing 0,
\lambda_{+,a}(n,\delta)
and
\lambda_{-,a}(n,\delta)
be the maximum and minimum of the eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi_{a}(\lambda)
on
\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n,\delta)
.
Let
s>1
and let
\zeta
be the Riemann
\zeta
function.
For all
t
, let
i^{a}(t)=\lfloor\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a}\rfloor
.
With probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
, if for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
,
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(N_{t,a},\delta)
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d|\mathcal{S}|\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{\log\left(\frac{\zeta(s)^{|\mathcal{S}|}}{\delta}\right)}{d|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{s}{d|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log(1+\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a})+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)})\right)\>.

Proof.

Suppose that for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
,
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
. For all
(i^{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{N}^{|\mathcal{S}|}
, Lemma
41
gives that with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{\zeta(s)^{|\mathcal{S}|}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{S}}(i^{a}+1)^{s}}
, if for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
,
N_{t,a}\in[n_{i^{a}},n_{i^{a}+1})
and
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n_{i^{a}},\delta)
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d|\mathcal{S}|\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{d|\mathcal{S}|}\log\frac{\zeta(s)^{|\mathcal{S}|}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{S}}(i^{a}+1)^{s}}{\delta}+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i^{a}},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i^{a}},\delta)})\right)\>.

With probability
1-\delta
, this is true for all
(i^{a})_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\in\mathbb{N}^{|\mathcal{S}|}
. In particular we have the inequality for
(i^{a}(t))_{a\in\mathcal{S}}
: if for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
,
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})

\displaystyle\leq d|\mathcal{S}|\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{1}{d|\mathcal{S}|}\log\frac{\zeta(s)^{|\mathcal{S}|}\prod_{a\in\mathcal{S}}(i^{a}(t)+1)^{s}}{\delta}+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)})\right)\>.

Since the sets
A_{a}(n,\delta)
are non-increasing with respect to
n
, a sufficient condition for
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)
is
\theta_{N_{t,a},a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(N_{t,a},\delta)
.
By definition of
i^{a}(t)
, it satisfies
i^{a}(t)\leq\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a}
. We get the inequality of the theorem.
∎

F.3
Univariate Gaussian

Theorem
43
gives a time-uniform upper tail concentration of the sum of
\mathcal{S}
KL divergence of Gaussian with unknown variance. It is obtained by using Theorem
42
with adequate pre-concentration results.

Theorem 43
.

For
i\in\{0,-1\}
, let
\overline{W}_{i}(x)=-W_{i}(-e^{-x})
for
x\geq 1
,
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\mathcal{S}\in\subseteq[K]
,
\eta_{0}>0
,
\eta_{1}>0
,
\gamma>1
,
s>1
and
\zeta
be the Riemann
\zeta
function. Let

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu}(t,\delta)=\frac{1}{t}\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+2\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+2s+2s\log\left(1+\frac{\log t}{2s}\right)\right)\>,

\displaystyle 1+\varepsilon_{+,\sigma}(t,\delta)=\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{2(1+\eta_{1})}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta_{1})}\right)\right)\right)-\frac{1}{t}\>,

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon_{-,\sigma}(t,\delta)=\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{2(1+\eta_{0})}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta_{0})}\right)\right)\right)-\frac{1}{t}\>.

For all
a\in[K]
and
t\geq t_{a}(\delta)
, let
i_{a}(t)=\lfloor\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a}\rfloor
and
\bar{t}_{a}=\inf\left\{t\mid N_{t,a}\geq i_{a}(t)\right\}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{\pm,t,a}=\mu_{\bar{t}_{a},a}\pm 2\sigma_{\bar{t}_{a},a}\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon_{\mu}(N_{\bar{t}_{a},a},\delta)}{1-\varepsilon_{-,\sigma}(N_{\bar{t}_{a},a}-1,\delta)}}\quad\text{,}\quad\sigma^{2}_{\pm,t,a}=\sigma_{\bar{t}_{a},a}^{2}\frac{1\pm\varepsilon_{\pm,\sigma}(N_{\bar{t}_{a},a}-1,\delta)}{1\mp\varepsilon_{\mp,\sigma}(N_{\bar{t}_{a},a}-1,\delta)}\>,

\displaystyle\mu_{++,t,a}^{2}=\max\mu_{\pm,t,a}^{2}\quad\text{,}\quad R_{t,a}(\delta)=\frac{\sigma^{3}_{+,t,a}f_{+}\left(g(\sigma^{2}_{+,t,a},\mu_{++,t,a}^{2})\right)}{\sigma^{3}_{-,t,a}f_{-}\left(g(\sigma^{2}_{-,t,a},\mu_{++,t,a}^{2})\right)}\>,

where
f_{\pm}(x)=\frac{1\pm\sqrt{1-x}}{\sqrt{x}}
and
g(x,y)=\frac{2x}{(x+2y+\frac{1}{2})^{2}}
. Then, with probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\geq\max_{a\in\mathcal{S}}t_{a}(\delta)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}\KL((\mu_{t,a},\sigma_{t,a}^{2}),(\mu_{a},\sigma^{2}_{a}))

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathcal{S}|\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{2\zeta(s)^{|\mathcal{S}|}}{\delta}}{2|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{s}{2|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log(1+\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a})+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log\left(\gamma R_{t,a}(\delta)\right)\right)\>.

For all
a\in\mathcal{S}
, the stochastic initial time is defined as

\displaystyle t_{a}(\delta)=\inf\left\{t\mid N_{t,a}>1+\max\left\{t_{\pm}(\delta),e^{1+W_{0}\left(\frac{2(1+\eta_{0})}{e}\left(\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(N_{t,a}-1)}{\log(1+\eta_{0})}\right)\right)-e^{-1}\right)}\right\}\right\}\>.

(16)

where
\log(t_{-}(\delta))=\frac{s}{\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)}-\log(1+\eta_{0})
and
\log(t_{+}(\delta))=\frac{s}{\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)-\frac{1}{2(1+\eta_{1})}}-\log(1+\eta_{1})
.

As
W_{0}(x)\in[-1,+\infty)
,
t\geq t_{a}(\delta)
implies that
N_{t,a}>2
. Numerically, we always observed
\max t_{\pm}(\delta)<2
.

The distribution family
\mathcal{D}
of Gaussian distributions with positive variance is an exponential family, with natural parameter domain
\Theta_{D}=\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\star}_{-}
and log-partition function
\phi(\theta)=-\frac{\theta_{1}^{2}}{4\theta_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\log(-2\theta_{2})
.
The distribution with mean
x
and variance
v
corresponds to natural parameters
(\frac{x}{v},-\frac{1}{2v})
.
The distribution with natural parameter
\theta_{a}
has mean
\mu_{a}=-\frac{\theta_{a,1}}{2\theta_{a,2}}
and variance
\sigma_{a}^{2}=-\frac{1}{2\theta_{a,2}}
.
With those correspondences, we get
\phi(\theta_{a})=\frac{\mu_{a}^{2}}{2\sigma_{a}^{2}}+\log\sigma_{a}
and
\nabla\phi(\theta_{a})=(\mu_{a},\mu_{a}^{2}+\sigma_{a}^{2})
.
The domain
\nabla\phi(\Theta_{D})
is the set
\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\mid y>x^{2}\}
.
Finally, the sufficient statistic for that exponential family is
F(X)=(X,X^{2})
.
Indeed we have

\displaystyle d\nu_{\mu_{a},\sigma_{a}^{2}}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-(x-\mu_{a})^{2}/2\sigma_{a}^{2}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{\theta_{a}^{\top}(x,x^{2})-\phi(\theta_{a})}\>.

We define the estimator
F_{t,a}=\frac{1}{N_{t,a}}\sum_{s=1}^{t}F(X_{s})\mathbf{1}\{a_{s}=a\}=(\mu_{t,a},\mu_{t,a}^{2}+\sigma_{t,a}^{2})
.
When
\sigma_{t,a}^{2}>0
,
F_{t,a}
belongs to
\nabla\phi(\Theta_{D})
and the maximum likelihood estimator for
\theta_{a}
in
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{\star}_{-}
is well defined: it is
\theta_{t,a}\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}\nabla\phi^{-1}(F_{t,a})
. With probability 1, we have
\sigma_{t,a}^{2}>0
if and only if
N_{t,a}>1
. That maximum likelihood estimator (MLE) has mean
\mu_{t,a}
and variance
\sigma^{2}_{t,a}
. Therefore, the condition
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
will be satisfied almost surely if and only if
N_{t,a}>1
.

Preliminary concentration

We will need non-increasing sequence of convex sets
A_{a}(N_{t,a},\delta)
containing
0
such that with probability
1-\delta
, for all
t
greater than an initial time
t_{0}(\delta)
, the MLE
\theta_{t,a}
belongs to
\theta_{a}+A_{a}(N_{t,a},\delta)
. Thanks to Lemma
44
, the problem is reduced to the task of finding intervals for the mean and variance. The non-increasingness of the sequence of convex sets will be a direct consequence of the monotonicity of the bounds defining the rectangles
[\mu_{-,a},\mu_{+,a}]\times[\sigma_{-,a}^{2},\sigma_{+,a}^{2}]
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
(Lemma
46
).

Lemma 44
.

A rectangle in mean and variance space maps to a (convex) trapeze in natural parameter space. That is, for
\sigma_{-}^{2}>0
the set
[\mu_{-},\mu_{+}]\times[\sigma_{-}^{2},\sigma_{+}^{2}]
represents the same distributions as the natural parameters
\{\tilde{\theta}\in\Theta_{D}\mid\tilde{\theta}_{2}\in[-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\sigma}_{-}^{2}},-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\sigma}_{+}^{2}}],\>\frac{\tilde{\theta}_{1}}{\tilde{\theta}_{2}}\in[-2\tilde{\mu}_{+},-2\tilde{\mu}_{-}]\}
. If
(\mu,\sigma^{2})\in[\mu_{-},\mu_{+}]\times[\sigma_{-}^{2},\sigma_{+}^{2}]
, it rewrites as
\theta+A
, where
A
is a (convex) trapeze containing
0_{2}
.

Proof.

Given
(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\sigma}^{2})
, the associated natural parameter is
\tilde{\theta}=\left(\frac{\tilde{\mu}}{\tilde{\sigma}^{2}},-\frac{1}{2\tilde{\sigma}^{2}}\right)
. In particular, for
\theta=(\mu,\sigma^{2})
, we define
A
to be the following trapeze in the natural parameter space

\displaystyle\theta+A

\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}\left\{\tilde{\theta}\mid(\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\sigma}^{2})\in[\mu_{-},\mu_{+}]\times[\sigma^{2}_{-},\sigma_{+}^{2}]\right\}

\displaystyle=\>\left\{\tilde{\theta}\mid\tilde{\theta}_{2}\in\left[-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{-}^{2}},-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{+}^{2}}\right],\>\tilde{\theta}_{1}\in\left[-2\mu_{-}\tilde{\theta}_{2},-2\mu_{+}\tilde{\theta}_{2}\right]\right\}.

where the second equality uses that
\tilde{\theta}_{2}<0
. Since convexity is preserved by translation,
A
is convex if and only if
\theta+A
is convex, which is direct by considering the last expression. Likewise,
A
is a trapeze since
\theta+A
and geometry is preserved by translation. By assumption, we have
(\mu,\sigma^{2})\in[\mu_{-},\mu_{+}]\times[\sigma_{-}^{2},\sigma_{+}^{2}]
. Therefore,
\theta\in\theta+A
, in other words
A
contains
0_{2}
. Note that
A\subset\left[\frac{\mu_{-}}{\sigma_{+}^{2}},\frac{\mu_{+}}{\sigma_{-}^{2}}\right]\times\left[-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{-}^{2}},-\frac{1}{2\sigma_{+}^{2}}\right]
.
∎

Combining the time-uniform concentration inequalities for the mean (Lemma
28
) and the variance (Corollary
26
) derived in Appendix
E
, a direct union bound yields that with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
, for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
and all
t\geq t_{a}(\delta)

\displaystyle\sigma_{a}^{2}\left(1-\varepsilon_{-,\sigma}(N_{t,a}-1,\delta)\right)\leq\sigma^{2}_{t,a}\leq\sigma_{a}^{2}\left(1+\varepsilon_{+,\sigma}(N_{t,a}-1,\delta)\right)\>,

\displaystyle|\mu_{t,a}-\mu_{a}|\leq\sigma_{a}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mu}(N_{t,a},\delta)}\>,

where, abusing notations compared to Lemma
5
(re-scaling of
\delta
),

\displaystyle t_{a}(\delta)=\inf\left\{t\mid N_{t,a}>1+e^{1+W_{0}\left(\frac{2(1+\eta_{0})}{e}\left(\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(N_{t,a}-1)}{\log(1+\eta_{0})}\right)\right)-e^{-1}\right)}\right\}\>,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu}(t,\delta)=\frac{1}{t}\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+2\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+2s+2s\log\left(1+\frac{\log t}{2s}\right)\right)\>,

\displaystyle 1+\varepsilon_{+,\sigma}(t,\delta)=\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{2(1+\eta_{1})}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta_{1})}\right)\right)\right)-\frac{1}{t}\>,

\displaystyle 1-\varepsilon_{-,\sigma}(t,\delta)=\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+\frac{2(1+\eta_{0})}{t}\left(\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+s\log\left(1+\frac{\log(t)}{\log(1+\eta_{0})}\right)\right)\right)-\frac{1}{t}\>.

Note that
t\geq t_{a}(\delta)
implies that
\sigma^{2}_{t,a}>0
and
N_{t,a}>2
hence
F_{N_{t,a},a}\in\nabla\phi_{a}(\Theta_{D,a})
. Defining

\displaystyle\mu_{\pm,t,a}=\mu_{a}\pm\sigma_{a}\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mu}(N_{t,a},\delta)}\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma^{2}_{\pm,t,a}=\sigma_{a}^{2}\left(1\pm\varepsilon_{\pm,\sigma}(N_{t,a}-1,\delta)\right)\>,

we have obtain non-decreasing sequences
\mu_{-,t,a}
and
\sigma^{2}_{-,t,a}
, and non-increasing sequences
\mu_{+,t,a}
and
\sigma^{2}_{+,t,a}
, such that with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
, for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
and all
t\geq t_{a}(\delta)
,

(\mu_{a},\sigma_{a}^{2})\in[\mu_{-,t,a},\mu_{+,t,a}]\times[\sigma^{2}_{-,t,a},\sigma^{2}_{+,t,a}]\quad\text{and}\quad(\mu_{t,a},\sigma_{t,a}^{2})\in[\mu_{-,t,a},\mu_{+,t,a}]\times[\sigma^{2}_{-,t,a},\sigma^{2}_{+,t,a}]\>.

For all
a\in\mathcal{S}
, applying Lemma
44
on this non-increasing sequence of rectangles yield a sequence of non-increasing convex sets containing
0_{2}
, denoted by
(A_{a}(n,\delta))_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
, such that
\theta_{a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(n,\delta)
. By construction, we have with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{2}
, for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
and all
t\geq t_{a}(\delta)
,

\displaystyle\theta_{t,a}\in\theta_{a}+A_{a}(N_{t,a},\delta)\>.

This concludes the construction of the preliminary concentration sets used to apply Theorem
42
. Doing a union bound (splitting
\delta
in two) and restricting to
t\geq\max_{a\in\mathcal{S}}t_{a}(\delta)
, we obtain that with probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\geq\max_{a\in\mathcal{S}}t_{a}(\delta)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}N_{t,a}\KL((\mu_{t,a},\sigma_{t,a}^{2}),(\mu_{a},\sigma^{2}_{a}))

\displaystyle\leq 2|\mathcal{S}|\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+\frac{\log\frac{2\zeta(s)^{|\mathcal{S}|}}{\delta}}{2|\mathcal{S}|}+\frac{s}{2|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log(1+\log_{\gamma}N_{t,a})+\frac{1}{|\mathcal{S}|}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{S}}\log\left(\gamma\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)}\right)\right)\>.

where we used that
d_{\phi_{a}}(\theta_{a},\theta_{N_{t,a},a})=\KL((\mu_{t,a},\sigma_{t,a}^{2}),(\mu_{a},\sigma^{2}_{a}))
.

Controlling the eigenvalues

While the above is enough to obtain a concentration result, the ratio
\frac{\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)}{\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)}
cannot be computed since it depends on
(\mu,\sigma^{2})
. To circumvent this issue, we derive an upper bound on this unknown quantity, which will yield a valid concentration inequality.

Upper bounding this quantity might be done by considering a larger rectangle containing the one used to apply Theorem
42
, which can be done by chaining the concentration to replace
(\mu_{a},\sigma^{2}_{a})
by
(\mu_{t,a},\sigma^{2}_{t,a})
. Defining

\displaystyle\bar{\mu}_{\pm,t,a}=\mu_{t,a}\pm 2\sigma_{t,a}\sqrt{\frac{\varepsilon_{\mu}(N_{t,a},\delta)}{1-\varepsilon_{-,\sigma}(N_{t,a}-1,\delta)}}\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{\pm,t,a}=\sigma_{t,a}^{2}\frac{1\pm\varepsilon_{\pm,\sigma}(N_{t,a}-1,\delta)}{1\mp\varepsilon_{\mp,\sigma}(N_{t,a}-1,\delta)}\>,

we have by direct manipulations that

\displaystyle[\mu_{-,t,a},\mu_{+,t,a}]\times[\sigma^{2}_{-,t,a},\sigma^{2}_{+,t,a}]\subset[\bar{\mu}_{-,t,a},\bar{\mu}_{+,t,a}]\times[\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{-,t,a},\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{+,t,a}]\>.

By Lemma
44
, there is also inclusion of the associated trapeze in the natural space parameters, hence implying the following ordering of the eigenvalues

\displaystyle\lambda_{-,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)\geq\bar{\lambda}_{-,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{+,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)\leq\bar{\lambda}_{+,a}(n_{i^{a}(t)},\delta)\>.

Lemma
45
provides a control on the eigenvalues
\lambda_{+}
and
\lambda_{-}
in the case of Gaussian distributions based on the trapeze obtained in Lemma
44
.

Lemma 45
.

Let
\mu_{-}
and
\mu_{+}
be real values such that
\mu_{-}<\mu_{+}
.
Let
\sigma_{-}^{2}
and
\sigma_{+}^{2}
be real values such that
0<\sigma_{-}^{2}<\sigma_{+}^{2}
.
Let
\Theta
be the trapeze corresponding to that set in natural parameter space (see Lemma
44
).
The minimal and maximal eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}\phi(\lambda)
on
\Theta
are

\displaystyle\lambda_{-}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{2}\sigma_{-}^{3}f_{-}\left(g(\sigma_{-}^{2},\mu_{++}^{2})\right)\>,

\displaystyle\lambda_{+}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\sigma_{+}^{3}f_{+}\left(g(\sigma_{+}^{2},\mu_{++}^{2})\right)\>,

where
\mu_{++}^{2}=\max\{\mu_{-}^{2},\mu_{+}^{2}\}
,
f_{\pm}(x)=\frac{1\pm\sqrt{1-x}}{\sqrt{x}}
and
g(x,y)=\frac{2x}{(x+2y+\frac{1}{2})^{2}}
.

Proof.

Recall that
\phi(\theta)=-\frac{\theta_{1}^{2}}{4\theta_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}\log(-2\theta_{2})
, therefore we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla\phi(\theta)=\begin{bmatrix}-\frac{\theta_{1}}{2\theta_{2}}\\
\left(\frac{\theta_{1}}{2\theta_{2}}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{2\theta_{2}}\end{bmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla^{2}\phi(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\theta_{2}^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}-\theta_{2}&\theta_{1}\\
\theta_{1}&1-\frac{\theta_{1}^{2}}{\theta_{2}}\end{bmatrix}\>.

Computing the eigenvalues of
\nabla\phi(\theta)
, we have
\lambda_{-}(\theta)I_{2}\preccurlyeq\nabla^{2}\phi(\theta)\preccurlyeq\lambda_{+}(\theta)I_{2}
for all
\theta\in\Theta_{D}
, where the values of the eigenvalues expressed with the mean parameters are

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pm}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}\left(\sigma^{2}+2\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\pm\sqrt{\left(\sigma^{2}+2\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}-2\sigma^{2}}\right)\>.

Note that
\left(\sigma^{2}+2\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}-2\sigma^{2}\geq 0\iff(\sigma^{2})^{2}+(4\mu^{2}-1)\sigma^{2}+(2\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2})^{2}
. Since
(4\mu^{2}-1)^{2}-4(2\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2})^{2}=-16\mu^{2}\leq 0
, we have
\left(\sigma^{2}+2\mu^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}-2\sigma^{2}\geq 0
.

Defining
f_{\pm}(x)=\frac{1\pm\sqrt{1-x}}{\sqrt{x}}
and
g(x,y)=\frac{2x}{(x+2y+\frac{1}{2})^{2}}
, we have, for all
\tilde{\theta}\in\theta+A
,

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pm}(\tilde{\theta})

\displaystyle=\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\left(\tilde{\sigma}^{2}+2\tilde{\mu}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\pm\sqrt{\left(\tilde{\sigma}^{2}+2\tilde{\mu}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{2}-2\tilde{\sigma}^{2}}\right)=\sqrt{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{3}f_{\pm}\left(g(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\tilde{\mu}^{2})\right)\>.

Direct computations yield, for all
x\in(0,1)

\displaystyle f_{+}^{\prime}(x)=-\frac{1+\sqrt{1-x}}{2x\sqrt{x(1-x)}}<0\quad\text{and}\quad f_{-}^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-x}}{2x\sqrt{x(1-x)}}>0\>,

hence
f_{+}
is decreasing on
(0,1)
and
f_{-}
is increasing on
(0,1)
. For all
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}

\displaystyle\partial_{y}g(x,y)=-\frac{8x}{(x+2y+\frac{1}{2})^{3}}\leq 0\>,

hence
y\mapsto g(x,y)
is decreasing for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Let
\mu_{++}^{2}=\max\{\mu_{-}^{2},\mu_{+}^{2}\}
. By composition rule and
\lambda_{\pm}(\tilde{\theta})=\sqrt{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{3}f_{\pm}\left(g(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\tilde{\mu}^{2})\right)
, we obtain that: for all
\tilde{\theta}\in\theta+A(n,\delta)

\displaystyle\lambda_{+}(\tilde{\theta})\leq\sqrt{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{3}f_{+}\left(g(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\mu_{++}^{2})\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\lambda_{-}(\tilde{\theta})\geq\sqrt{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{3}f_{-}\left(g(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\mu_{++}^{2})\right)\>.

To show that
\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\mapsto\tilde{\sigma}^{3}f_{+}\left(g(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\mu_{++}^{2})\right)
and
\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\mapsto\tilde{\sigma}^{3}f_{-}\left(g(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\mu_{++}^{2})\right)
are increasing on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
, we use
\tilde{\sigma}^{3}f_{\pm}\left(g(\tilde{\sigma}^{2},\mu_{++}^{2})\right)=\tilde{\sigma}^{2}f_{2\mu_{++}^{2}+\frac{1}{2},\pm}(\tilde{\sigma}^{2})=h_{a,\pm}(\tilde{\sigma}^{2})
where
f_{a,\pm}(x)=x+a\pm\sqrt{\left(x+a\right)^{2}-2x}\geq 0
and
h_{a,\pm}(x)=xf_{a,\pm}(x)
. Since
h_{a,\pm}^{\prime}(x)=f_{a,\pm}(x)+xf_{a,\pm}^{\prime}(x)
and
f_{a,\pm}(x)\geq 0
, having
f_{a,\pm}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
is sufficient to conclude that
h_{a,\pm}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
. Therefore, a sufficient condition to conclude is to show that
f_{a,\pm}
is increasing on
\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

\displaystyle f_{a,\pm}^{\prime}(x)=1\pm\frac{x+a-1}{\sqrt{(x+a)^{2}-2x}}

When
x+a-1\geq 0
, we have directly
f_{a,+}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
. Moreover,

\displaystyle f_{a,-}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0\iff 1\geq\frac{x+a-1}{\sqrt{(x+a)^{2}-2x}}\iff 0\geq 1-2a

Since
1-2a=-2\mu_{++}^{2}
, we can conclude that
f_{a,-}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
.

When
x+a-1<0
, we have directly
f_{a,-}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
. Moreover,

\displaystyle f_{a,+}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0\iff 1\geq-\frac{x+a-1}{\sqrt{(x+a)^{2}-2x}}\iff 0\geq 1-2a

Since
1-2a=-2\mu_{++}^{2}
, we can conclude that
f_{a,-}^{\prime}(x)\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma
45
and using the above arguments concludes the proof of Theorem
43
.

Monotonicity of the preliminary concentration

The non-increasingness of the sequence of convex sets is obtained by monotonicity of the bounds of the rectangles
[\mu_{-,a},\mu_{+,a}]\times[\sigma_{-,a}^{2},\sigma_{+,a}^{2}]
for all
a\in\mathcal{S}
.
Given their definitions, it is sufficient to show that the functions
t\mapsto\varepsilon_{\mu}(t,\delta)
and
t\mapsto\varepsilon_{\pm,\sigma}(t,\delta)
are decreasing.
This is shown in Lemma
46
. The conditions on the initial time to have monotonicity are quite mild since
t_{\pm}(\delta)<1
after a relatively mild condition on
\delta
. Depending on the choice of
(|\mathcal{S}|,s,\eta_{0},\eta_{1})
, it can even hold for all
\delta\in(0,1]
. Numerically, for any practical choices of parameters, we always had
t_{\pm}(\delta)<2
. Since
t\geq t_{a}(\delta)
implies that
N_{t,a}\geq 2
, the condition
N_{t,a}\geq t_{\pm}(\delta)
is milder.

Lemma 46
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1]
. The function
t\mapsto\varepsilon_{\mu}(t,\delta)
is decreasing on
[1,+\infty)
. There exists
t_{+}(\delta)
such that the function
t\mapsto\varepsilon_{\pm,\sigma}(t,\delta)
is decreasing on
[t_{+}(\delta),+\infty)
. In particular,
t_{-}(\delta)\leq 1
if and only if
\delta\leq 6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)e^{-\frac{s}{\log(1+\eta_{0})}}
and
t_{+}(\delta)\leq 1
if and only if
\delta\leq 6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)e^{-\frac{s}{\log(1+\eta_{1})}-\frac{1}{2(1+\eta_{1})}}
.

Proof.

Let
\delta\in(0,1]
. Recall that
\overline{W}_{i}^{\prime}(x)=\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{\overline{W}_{i}(x)}}
for all
x>1
and
i\in\{-1,0\}
(Lemma
75
).

Decreasing
\varepsilon_{\mu}
.
Let
f(t)=2s\log\left(1+\frac{\log t}{2s}\right)
and
c=1+2\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)+2s
. Since
s>1
and
\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}>1
, we have
c>3
. Directly, we have
f^{\prime}(t)=\frac{1}{t}\frac{1}{1+\frac{\log t}{2s}}
. Then, by composition of the derivatives, we obtain

\displaystyle t^{2}\frac{\partial\varepsilon_{\mu}(t,\delta)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\left(1+\frac{\log t}{2s}\right)\left(\overline{W}_{-1}\left(c+f(t)\right)-1\right)}{\left(1+\frac{\log t}{2s}\right)\left(1-\frac{1}{\overline{W}_{-1}\left(c+f(t)\right)}\right)}\>.

Since
t\geq 1
and
\overline{W}_{-1}\left(c+f(t)\right)>1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\varepsilon_{\mu}(t,\delta)}{\partial t}<0

\displaystyle\iff\left(1+\frac{\log t}{2s}\right)\left(\overline{W}_{-1}\left(c+f(t)\right)-1\right)>1

\displaystyle\iff\frac{\log t}{2s}>\frac{1}{\overline{W}_{-1}\left(c+f(t)\right)-1}-1\>.

Using that
f(t)\geq 0
for all
t\geq 1
,
c>3
and
\overline{W}_{-1}(x)>x
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\overline{W}_{-1}\left(c+f(t)\right)-1}-1\leq-\frac{1}{2}<0\leq\frac{\log t}{2s}\>.

Therefore, we have shown that
t\mapsto\varepsilon_{\mu}(t,\delta)
is decreasing on
[1,+\infty)
for all
\delta\in(0,1]
.

Decreasing
\varepsilon_{\pm,\sigma}
.
Let
(i_{+},i_{-})=(-1,0)
and
(\eta_{+},\eta_{-})=(\eta_{1},\eta_{0})
. Let
a_{\pm}=2(1+\eta_{\pm})
,
b=\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)
,
f_{\pm}(t)=s\log\left(1+\frac{\log t}{\log(1+\eta_{\pm})}\right)
and
g_{\pm}(t)=\frac{b+f_{\pm}(t)}{t}
. Directly, we have

f_{\pm}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{1}{t}\frac{s}{\log(1+\eta_{\pm})+\log t}\quad\text{and}\quad g_{\pm}^{\prime}(t)=\frac{tf_{\pm}^{\prime}(t)-(b+f_{\pm}(t))}{t^{2}}\>.

Then, by composition of the derivatives, we obtain

\displaystyle\pm t^{2}\frac{\partial\varepsilon_{\pm,\sigma}(t,\delta)}{\partial t}

\displaystyle=\frac{\overline{W}_{i_{\pm}}\left(1+a_{\pm}g_{\pm}(t)\right)\left(1-a_{\pm}\left(b+f_{\pm}(t)-tf_{\pm}^{\prime}(t)\right)\right)-1}{1-\overline{W}_{i_{\pm}}\left(1+a_{\pm}g_{\pm}(t)\right)}\>.

Since
t\geq 1
,
\overline{W}_{-1}\left(1+a_{+}g_{+}(t)\right)>1
and
\overline{W}_{0}\left(1+a_{-}g_{-}(t)\right)<1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\varepsilon_{\pm,\sigma}(t,\delta)}{\partial t}<0

\displaystyle\iff a_{\pm}\left(b+f_{\pm}(t)-tf_{\pm}^{\prime}(t)\right)>1-\frac{1}{\overline{W}_{i_{\pm}}\left(1+a_{\pm}g_{\pm}(t)\right)}

\displaystyle\impliedby a_{\pm}\left(b-\frac{s}{\log(1+\eta_{\pm})+\log t}\right)\geq 1-\frac{1}{\overline{W}_{i_{\pm}}\left(1+a_{\pm}g_{\pm}(t)\right)}\>,

where the sufficient condition is obtained by noting that
f_{\pm}(t)>0
for
t>1
. Using that
\overline{W}_{0}(1+x)\in(0,1)
and that
\overline{W}_{-1}(1+x)>1
, we can obtain further sufficient conditions

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\varepsilon_{+,\sigma}(t,\delta)}{\partial t}<0

\displaystyle\impliedby\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2(1+\eta_{1})}+\frac{s}{\log(1+\eta_{1})+\log t}

\displaystyle\iff t\geq t_{+}(\delta)=\exp\left(\frac{s}{\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)-\frac{1}{2(1+\eta_{1})}}-\log(1+\eta_{1})\right)\>,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\varepsilon_{-,\sigma}(t,\delta)}{\partial t}<0

\displaystyle\impliedby t\geq t_{-}(\delta)=\exp\left(\frac{s}{\log\left(\frac{6|\mathcal{S}|\zeta(s)}{\delta}\right)}-\log(1+\eta_{0})\right)\>.

∎

Appendix G
Thresholds

After leveraging the link between the GLR and the EV-GLR statistics (Lemma
47
) and showing how to calibrate
\delta
-correct thresholds (Lemma
48
), we derive several
\delta
-correct family of thresholds with increasing complexities both theoretically and numerically: Student thresholds (Appendix
G.1
), box thresholds (Appendix
G.2
), KL thresholds (Appendix
G.3
) and BoB thresholds (Appendix
G.4
).
In Appendix
G.5
, we study whether the derived family of thresholds is asymptotically tight.

Thresholds Relationship

Lemma
47
shows that the relationship between the GLR and the EV-GLR statistics (Lemma
16
) allows to obtain
\delta
-correct thresholds for the EV-GLR stopping rule by using the ones obtained for GLR stopping rule, and vice-versa.

Lemma 47
.

Let
(c_{a,b})_{(a,b)\in[K]^{2}}
be a family of thresholds.

If
(c_{a,b})_{(a,b)\in[K]^{2}}
ensures
\delta
-correctness of the EV-GLR stopping rule, then it ensures
\delta
-correctness of the GLR stopping rule.

Let
C_{b}(\mu_{t},\sigma^{2}_{t})=\frac{(\mu_{t,\hat{a}_{t}}-\mu_{t,b})^{2}}{\min\{\sigma_{t,\hat{a}_{t}}^{2},\sigma_{t,b}^{2}\}}
. If
(c_{a,b})_{(a,b)\in[K]^{2}}
ensures
\delta
-correctness of the GLR stopping rule, then
(\tilde{c}_{a,b})_{(a,b)\in[K]^{2}}
ensures
\delta
-correctness of the EV-GLR stopping rule, where
\tilde{c}_{a,b}(N_{t},\delta)=\frac{C_{b}(\mu_{t},\sigma^{2}_{t})}{\log\left(1+C_{b}(\mu_{t},\sigma^{2}_{t})\right)}c_{a,b}(N_{t},\delta)
.

Proof.

Using Lemma
16
, we have the following inequalities between the statistics involved in the GLR and EV-GLR stopping rules

Z^{\text{EV}}_{a}(t)\geq Z_{a}(t)\geq\frac{\log\left(1+C_{a}(\mu_{t},\sigma^{2}_{t})\right)}{C_{a}(\mu_{t},\sigma^{2}_{t})}Z^{\text{EV}}_{a}(t)\>.

Let
(c_{a,b})_{(a,b)\in[K]^{2}}
a family of thresholds ensuring
\delta
-correctness of
\tau_{\delta}^{\text{EV}}
. We show by inclusion of event that
(c_{a,b})_{(a,b)\in[K]^{2}}
is a family of thresholds ensuring
\delta
-correctness of
\tau_{\delta}
.

\displaystyle\left\{\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\right\}

\displaystyle=\left\{\exists t\in\mathbb{N},\>\forall a\neq\hat{a}_{t},\>Z_{a}(t)>c_{\hat{a}_{t},a}(N_{t},\delta),\hat{a}_{t}\neq a^{\star}\right\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\left\{\exists t\in\mathbb{N},\>\forall a\neq\hat{a}_{t},\>\>Z^{\text{EV}}_{a}(t)>c_{\hat{a}_{t},a}(N_{t},\delta),\hat{a}_{t}\neq a^{\star}\right\}

The exact same argument can be used to show the second statement.
∎

Calibration by concentration

Lemma
48
gives the terms to concentrate to ensure
\delta
-correctness.
Due to the structure of the identification problem which doesn’t involve the variance, two distance metrics can be used as starting point for the GLR stopping rule.
They both rely on a weighted sum of the per-arm KL divergences between the current estimator and an unknown parameter
(\mu,\tilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2})
.
While the choice
\tilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}=\sigma^{2}
seems natural in (
9
), it doesn’t fully leverage the BAI structure.
This can be done in (
6
) by choosing
\tilde{\sigma}_{t}^{2}=\sigma_{t}^{2}+(\mu_{t}-\mu)^{2}
, which yields smaller thresholds.

Lemma 48
.

If with probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
a\neq a^{\star}(\mu)
,

\sum_{b\in\{a,a^{\star}(\mu)\}}\frac{N_{t,b}}{2}\log\left(1+\frac{(\mu_{t,b}-\mu_{b})^{2}}{\sigma_{t,b}^{2}}\right)\leq c_{a,a^{\star}(\mu)}(N_{t},\delta)\>,

then the GLR stopping rule using the family of thresholds
(c_{b,a})_{(b,a)\in[K]^{2}}
is
\delta
-correct on
\mathcal{D}^{K}
.

If with probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
a\neq a^{\star}(\mu)
,

\sum_{b\in\{a,a^{\star}(\mu)\}}N_{t,b}\KL((\mu_{t,b},\sigma_{t,b}^{2}),(\mu_{b},\sigma^{2}_{b}))\leq c_{a,a^{\star}(\mu)}(N_{t},\delta)\>,

then the GLR stopping rule using the family of thresholds
(c_{b,a})_{(b,a)\in[K]^{2}}
is
\delta
-correct on
\mathcal{D}^{K}
.

If with probability
1-\delta
, for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
a\neq a^{\star}(\mu)
,

\sum_{b\in\{a,a^{\star}(\mu)\}}N_{t,b}\frac{(\mu_{t,b}-\mu_{b})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t,b}^{2}}\leq c_{a,a^{\star}(\mu)}(N_{t},\delta)\>,

(17)

then the EV-GLR stopping rule using the family of thresholds
(c_{b,a})_{(b,a)\in[K]^{2}}
is
\delta
-correct on
\mathcal{D}^{K}
.

Proof.

Let
\hat{a}_{t}=\hat{a}_{t}
and
a^{\star}=a^{\star}(\mu)
. Using Lemma
15
, the stopping time (
4
) for the GLR stopping rule involves the statistics

\displaystyle Z_{a}(t)

\displaystyle=\inf_{(\lambda,\kappa^{2}):\lambda_{a}\geq\lambda_{\hat{a}_{t}}}\sum_{b\in\{a,\hat{a}_{t}\}}N_{t,b}\KL((\mu_{t,b},\sigma_{t,b}^{2}),(\lambda_{b},\kappa_{b}^{2}))

\displaystyle=\inf_{(\lambda,\kappa^{2}):\lambda_{a}\geq\lambda_{\hat{a}_{t}}}\sum_{b\in\{a,\hat{a}_{t}\}}\frac{N_{t,b}}{2}\log\left(1+\frac{(\mu_{t,b}-\lambda_{b})^{2}}{\sigma_{t,b}^{2}}\right)\>.

Then, by definition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in\mathbb{N},\>\exists a\neq a^{\star},\>a=\hat{a}_{t},\>\forall c\neq a,\>Z_{c}(t)>c_{a,c}(N_{t},\delta)\right)\>.

Since a valid choice is
c=a^{\star}
and
\lambda=\mu
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\tau_{\delta}<+\infty,\hat{a}_{\tau_{\delta}}\neq a^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in\mathbb{N},\>\exists a\neq a^{\star},\>\sum_{b\in\{a^{\star},a\}}N_{t,b}\KL((\mu_{t,b},\sigma_{t,b}^{2}),(\mu_{b},\sigma_{b}^{2}))>c_{a,a^{\star}}(N_{t},\delta)\right)\\
\mathbb{P}\left(\exists t\in\mathbb{N},\>\exists a\neq a^{\star},\>\sum_{b\in\{a^{\star},a\}}\frac{N_{t,b}}{2}\log\left(1+\frac{(\mu_{t,b}-\mu_{b})^{2}}{\sigma_{t,b}^{2}}\right)>c_{a,a^{\star}}(N_{t},\delta)\right)\end{cases}\>.

The concentration assumptions yield
\delta
-correctness of the first two family of thresholds.
The proof for the EV-GLR stopping rule is identical to the one for the GLR stopping rule except that it uses the statistics
Z^{\text{EV}}_{a}(t)
.
∎

G.1
Student Thresholds

Lemma
49
gives the family of Student thresholds.

Lemma 49
.

Let
s>1
and
\zeta
be the Riemann
\zeta
function. Let a family of thresholds
c_{a,b}(N_{t},\delta)
with value
+\infty
if
t<\max_{c\in\{a,b\}}t_{c}^{\text{S}}(\delta)
and otherwise
c_{a,b}^{\text{S}}(N_{t},\delta)=\max\left\{\beta^{\text{S}}(N_{t,a},\delta),\beta^{\text{S}}(N_{t,b},\delta)\right\}
. Taking

\beta^{\text{S}}(t,\delta)=t\log\left(1+\frac{1}{t-1}Q\left(1-\frac{\delta}{4(K-1)\zeta(s)t^{s}};\mathcal{T}_{t-1}\right)^{2}\right)

(18)

yields a
\delta
